# Configuration

Core is a Spring Boot application: every property can be set in YAML or as an
environment variable (Spring's relaxed binding: `app.storage.type` becomes
`APP_STORAGE_TYPE`). The Compose tiers take their settings from
`deploy/compose/.env` (documented, grouped, generated by
`./scripts/vegaduta secrets`); Helm takes them from chart values.

Source of truth for defaults: `core/app-config/src/main/resources/application*.yml`.
Line numbers drift; search for the property name.

## Spring profiles

Profiles switch whole feature areas on or off at boot. Set them with
`SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE`.

| Profile | Turns on | File |
|---|---|---|
| `security` | Keycloak JWT resource server, admin client, RBAC | `application-security.yml` |
| `knowledge` | RAG on pgvector, embeddings, reranking, upload limits | `application-knowledge.yml` |
| `graph-memory` | graph memory on Apache AGE | (beans only) |
| `workflow` | Kafka/Redpanda workflow events, Telegram, workflow mail | `application-workflow.yml` |
| `temporal` | Temporal workflow engine client | (beans only) |
| `mcp` | static stdio MCP servers shared by all tenants (spawned by `mcp-stdio-runner` when `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_ENABLED=true`, else inside core, which then needs the `core-full` image) | `application-mcp.yml` |
| `mcpserver` | the platform's own MCP server at `/mcp` | `application-mcpserver.yml` |
| `lite` | single-process laptop mode: tracing off, local-disk storage, in-process workflow engine, 2 s Redis timeout, mail/Telegram without the `workflow` profile | `application-lite.yml` |
| `personal` | one person on one machine: local sign-in instead of Keycloak, taint tracking enforced, STDIO MCP servers under an OS sandbox, skill learning on, billing off, tracing off, local-disk storage, in-process workflow engine, small connection pools. See [Personal mode](personal-mode.md) and [Personal agent settings](#personal-agent-settings) | `application-personal.yml` |
| `shape-api` / `shape-jobs` | split deployment: the API skips scheduled work that the jobs shape owns | `application-shape-*.yml` |
| `shape-jobs-core` / `shape-jobs-ingest` | pools of the jobs shape: `ingest` runs only the knowledge pollers, `core` runs the rest. Activate after `shape-jobs`; see [Deployment shapes](deploy/DEPLOYMENT-SHAPES.md). The Helm chart now starts the main jobs pod as plain `shape-jobs` with `APP_SCHEDULING_OFFLOADED_POOLS`; `shape-jobs-core` stays as the hand-run equivalent of offloading `ingest` | `application-shape-jobs-*.yml` |
| `shape-jobs-pool` | a named pool of the jobs shape (`webhooks`, `intake`, `billing`), chosen by `APP_SCHEDULING_POOL`. Activate after `shape-jobs` | `application-shape-jobs-pool.yml` |
| `jobs` / `worker` | set by the `app-jobs` / `app-worker` shapes (Flyway off, own HTTP port) | in those modules |
| `e2e` | relaxed signup/abuse limits for the Playwright suite only | `application-e2e.yml` |

Defaults by where core runs:

| Where | `SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE` |
|---|---|
| `application.yml` (bare JVM) | `security,knowledge,workflow,graph-memory` |
| core image (`core/Dockerfile`, default `MCP_TOOLCHAIN=none`) | `security,knowledge,workflow,graph-memory` |
| core-full image (`MCP_TOOLCHAIN=full`) | `security,knowledge,workflow,graph-memory,mcp` |
| Lite (`deploy/compose/lite.yml`) | `security,knowledge,graph-memory,lite` (plus `,mcp` with `CORE_IMAGE_VARIANT=-full` if built-in STDIO servers should spawn in core instead of the runner) |
| Personal (`deploy/compose/personal.yml`, `scripts/vegaduta personal`) | `security,knowledge,graph-memory,personal` (override with `PERSONAL_SPRING_PROFILES`) |
| Full (`deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml`) | `security,knowledge,graph-memory,mcpserver` plus `CORE_MCP_PROFILE` (the CLI sets `,mcp` for the `tools`, `browser` and `sandbox` profiles) plus `CORE_PROFILES_EXTRA` (the CLI adds `,workflow` or `,workflow,temporal` for those profiles) |
| Helm (`charts/vegaduta`) | `global.core.springProfiles` (`security,knowledge,workflow,graph-memory,mcpserver`); `,mcp` is added when `global.components.mcpStdioRunner=true` (every core role) or `global.core.mcpToolchain=full` (API only) |

## Core shapes

One image, one reactor, four ways to run it. Build with
`docker build -f core/Dockerfile --build-arg MODULE=<module> core`.

| Shape | Module / main class | Role |
|---|---|---|
| api | `app` / `CoreApplication` | REST, SSE, WebSocket, MCP server, webhooks; the chat path |
| jobs | `app-jobs` / `JobsApplication` | scheduled sweeps and pollers; run exactly one |
| worker | `app-worker` / `WorkerApplication` | Temporal workflow worker |
| migrate | `app` with `app.tenancy.migrate-only=true` | runs Flyway for the control plane and every tenant schema, then exits; no `@Scheduled` job, Kafka consumer or Temporal worker runs in it |

`app.tenancy.migrate-on-boot` (default `true`) makes the API migrate tenant
schemas at start-up; set it to `false` when a separate migrate step runs (Helm
does this). Env names: `APP_TENANCY_MIGRATE_ON_BOOT`, `APP_TENANCY_MIGRATE_ONLY`.

The migrate shape exits 0 when every tenant is current, 1 when a tenant failed (the Job fails
the release) and 75 when another migrator holds the advisory lock `tenant-schema-migrate-only`
(a second concurrent Job; the Helm Job ignores the pod and starts a new one, without spending
`migrate.backoffLimit`). A 75 with no other migrator running is a bug, not a lock: report it.
Compose has no separate migrate step; core migrates on boot there.

## Essential vs plug-and-play services

The platform runs on a small essential set. Everything else is plug-and-play:
off by default, switched on per deployment, and core degrades cleanly when it
is absent. The default `core` image is slim (`core/Dockerfile`
`MCP_TOOLCHAIN=none`: JRE plus a bare `node` binary for the MCP relay, no
npm/npx, uv or browsers). The in-core toolchain that made the image about 6 GB
now lives in `mcp-stdio-runner` (and its `-browser` variant), or in the opt-in
`core-full` image.

| Service | Class | Enables | Compose (full tier) | Helm (`charts/vegaduta`) | Core flag |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| core API | essential | REST, SSE, WebSocket, chat path, MCP server at `/mcp` | always | always | — |
| web | essential | tenant console | always | `global.components.web` | — |
| postgres (pgvector + AGE) | essential | all data, RAG vectors, graph memory | always | `postgres.enabled` or `global.postgresql.host` | — |
| valkey | essential | cache, rate limits, SSE fan-out, MCP registry | always | `valkey.enabled` or `global.valkey.host` | — |
| keycloak | essential | sign-in, tenants, RBAC | always | `keycloak.enabled` or `global.oidc.issuerUrl` | — |
| core-jobs | scale shape | scheduled sweeps and pollers out of the API pods (API adds `shape-api`) | profile `jobs` | `global.components.coreJobs` (default on) | — |
| core-ingest | scale shape | pool of the jobs shape that runs only document ingestion and re-embedding | profile `shape-jobs-ingest` (core-jobs gets `APP_SCHEDULING_OFFLOADED_POOLS`) | `global.components.coreIngest` (default off, needs `coreJobs`) | — |
| core-webhooks | scale shape | pool of the jobs shape that runs only webhook delivery and the workflow event outbox relay | profile `shape-jobs-pool`, `APP_SCHEDULING_POOL=webhooks` | `global.components.coreWebhooks` (default off, needs `coreJobs`) | — |
| core-intake | scale shape | pool of the jobs shape that runs only the inbound email and X DM pollers | profile `shape-jobs-pool`, `APP_SCHEDULING_POOL=intake` | `global.components.coreIntake` (default off, needs `coreJobs`) | — |
| core-billing | scale shape | pool of the jobs shape that runs only the usage rollup, payment reconciliation and comp-grant sweeps | profile `shape-jobs-pool`, `APP_SCHEDULING_POOL=billing` | `global.components.coreBilling` (default off, needs `coreJobs`) | — |
| core-worker | scale shape | Temporal workflow worker | profile `temporal` | `global.temporal.enabled` | `WORKFLOW_EXECUTION_ENGINE=temporal` |
| migrate | scale shape | Flyway for every schema before the API starts | — (API migrates on boot) | `core.migrate.enabled` (hook Job) | `APP_TENANCY_MIGRATE_ON_BOOT=false` on the API |
| mcp-stdio-runner | plug-and-play | stdio MCP servers (`npx`/`uvx` packages): tenant-registered ones and the built-in `filesystem`, `memory`, `sequential-thinking` | profile `tools` (or `sandbox`) | `global.components.mcpStdioRunner` + `mcp-stdio-runner.runtimeClassName` | `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_ENABLED=true` + profile `mcp` |
| mcp-stdio-runner-browser | plug-and-play | the above plus the browser built-ins `puppeteer` and `playwright-vision` (pinned Chromium/Chrome baked in) | profile `browser` (`MCP_STDIO_RUNNER_VARIANT=-browser`) | `mcp-stdio-runner.browser=true` | same as the runner |
| core-full image | alternative | stdio MCP servers inside the core container, for hosts that cannot run the runner (single-user only: no isolation) | `CORE_IMAGE_VARIANT=-full` + `CORE_MCP_PROFILE=,mcp` | `global.core.mcpToolchain=full` (API only) | `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_ENABLED=false` |
| sidecar | plug-and-play | document ingestion, embeddings, rerank, STT/TTS, rendering | always in full; lite profile `rag` | `global.components.sidecar` (default on) | `SIDECAR_BASE_URL` |
| tool-sandbox | plug-and-play | tenant-authored Java/Python tools | profile `sandbox` | `global.components.toolSandbox` + RuntimeClass | `TOOL_SANDBOX_ENABLED=true` |
| code-intel | plug-and-play | code intelligence, repository indexing | profile `code-intel` | `global.components.codeIntel` | `CODE_INTEL_ENABLED=true` |
| app-builder | plug-and-play | the App Builder (`/build`) | profile `builder` | `global.components.appBuilder` | `APP_BUILDER_CORE_SERVICE_TOKEN` |
| voice-agent | plug-and-play | LiveKit voice calls with agents | profile `voice` | `global.components.voiceAgent` + `global.livekit.*` | `LIVEKIT_*` |
| rl-trainer | plug-and-play | offline reinforcement-learning training of agent policies | profile `rl` | `global.components.rlTrainer` | `RL_TRAINER_INTERNAL_TOKEN` |

How the `mcp` profile and the runner fit together:

- `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_ENABLED=true`: core relays every stdio MCP server, the
  static built-ins included (`StaticMcpStdioSidecarRouting`, same connection
  names and exposure policy), to the runner through the trusted
  `/opt/mcp-stdio-proxy/proxy.js`. `filesystem`'s root
  (`MCP_FILESYSTEM_ROOT`) then lives in the runner container. Browser tools
  work only when the browser image is behind the runner address.
- `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_ENABLED=false` with the `mcp` profile: core spawns the
  built-ins itself and needs `npx` on PATH (the `core-full` image). On the slim
  image core stops at startup with a message naming the three ways out: enable
  the runner, use `core-full`, or drop `mcp`.
- core-worker and core-jobs are published slim only. Give them MCP tools
  through the runner.

## Required secrets

| Variable | What | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` | AES-256-GCM key for stored provider keys, channel secrets, MCP env | **Base64 of exactly 32 bytes** (`openssl rand -base64 32`); core refuses to start otherwise. Must be **identical** for every core shape and every process that shares the database. Losing it makes the stored secrets unrecoverable: back it up with the database. |
| `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY_PREVIOUS` | previous key during rotation | optional |
| `PLATFORM_MASTER_KEY` | platform-level secret material | generated by the CLI |
| `POSTGRES_USER` / `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` (Compose `.env`) | PostgreSQL bootstrap **superuser** | used only by the one-shot `db-roles` service, backups and manual `psql`; never handed to core |
| `POSTGRES_APP_USER` / `POSTGRES_APP_PASSWORD` (Compose `.env`) | the login core uses | default `vegaduta_app`; owner of `POSTGRES_DB` (`agentic_ai`) but **not** a superuser. Compose passes it to core as `POSTGRES_USER`/`POSTGRES_PASSWORD`. Equal to `POSTGRES_USER` = opt out (not recommended) |
| `KEYCLOAK_CLIENT_SECRET` | secret of the `agentic-ai-core` client | must match the realm |

`scripts/vegaduta secrets` (and `scripts\vegaduta.ps1 secrets`) also keeps a copy of these values outside the install folder, readable only by you: `~/.config/vegaduta/secrets.env`, or `%LOCALAPPDATA%\VegaDuta\secrets.env` on Windows (also used from WSL). A new install folder reuses them, so the data Docker kept from an earlier install still opens. If Docker holds a personal install's data but no copy exists, the new install leaves that data untouched and starts under its own compose project (`VEGADUTA_PERSONAL_PROJECT`, e.g. `vegaduta-personal-2`).

| Variable | What | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `VEGADUTA_SECRETS_KEEP` (environment) | path of the kept copy | default above; tests point it at a temporary file |
| `VEGADUTA_PERSONAL_PROJECT` (Compose `.env`) | compose project of a personal install | set by `secrets` only in the case above; empty = `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME`, else `vegaduta-personal` |

## Database and cache

| Property | Env | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `spring.datasource.url` | `DB_HOST`, `DB_PORT`, `POSTGRES_DB` | `localhost:5432/agentic_ai` |
| Hikari pool | `DB_HIKARI_MAX_POOL_SIZE`, `DB_HIKARI_MIN_IDLE`, `DB_HIKARI_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT_MS` | 15, 5, 20000 |
| `app.tenancy.migrate-parallelism` | `APP_TENANCY_MIGRATE_PARALLELISM` | `4` tenant schemas migrated at once. Each Flyway run holds 2 pooled connections and the migrate Job holds 1 more for its advisory lock, so the value is capped to `(pool size - 2) / 2` (a warning says when it is). The Helm migrate Job's pool is 10, enough for 4 |
| `spring.data.redis.*` | `REDIS_HOST`, `REDIS_PORT`, `REDIS_PASSWORD` | `localhost`, `6379`, empty (Valkey is compatible) |
| `spring.data.redis.timeout` | `REDIS_TIMEOUT` | `5s` (Lite: `2s`); core issues no blocking Redis commands |

Postgres must have the **pgvector** extension. **Apache AGE** (graph memory) is
optional: the `ghcr.io/cd-lc/postgres` image has both; on a server that does not offer AGE (AWS RDS/Aurora,
Cloud SQL, AlloyDB) core records the graph-memory migration as skipped and you drop the `graph-memory` profile
(Helm: `global.postgresql.age=false`). A server that offers AGE but refuses it (not allow-listed, permission denied)
still fails the migration, so that is fixed rather than hidden. Each tenant is a schema selected with
`SET search_path`, so a PgBouncer in front of it **must use session pooling**
(`POOL_MODE=session`).

**Least-privileged login.** Run core as a role that owns the database but is
not a superuser (Compose does this for you, Helm's CloudNativePG cluster does it
too). That role needs, and only needs:

- ownership of the database (it creates one schema per tenant and runs Flyway);
- the `vector` and `age` extensions created beforehand by a superuser (neither
  is a "trusted" extension);
- `USAGE` on schema `ag_catalog` and `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` on its
  tables (graph memory);
- `age` in `shared_preload_libraries`. A non-superuser may only `LOAD`
  libraries under `$libdir/plugins/`; the `ghcr.io/cd-lc/postgres` image
  links `age.so` there, so `LOAD '$libdir/plugins/age'` works, while a plain
  `LOAD 'age'` is refused.

For an external database, create it that way yourself
(`deploy/postgres/ensure-app-role.sh` is the reference, run it as the
superuser with `PGHOST`, `PGUSER`, `PGDATABASE`, `APP_DB_USER`,
`APP_DB_PASSWORD` set).

### Database login without a stored password (IAM / token authentication)

`app.datasource.auth` (env `DB_AUTH`) selects how core logs in. The default,
`password`, is the behaviour above: `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` is required and Spring
Boot builds the pool. The other modes replace the password with a short-lived
token that is requested from the cloud for **every new physical connection**;
the same pool feeds the control-plane Flyway run, every per-tenant schema
migration, the migrate-only shape and JPA.

| `DB_AUTH` | Token | Credentials come from |
|---|---|---|
| `password` (default) | none | `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` |
| `aws-iam` | RDS / Aurora IAM auth token, valid 15 minutes (`RdsUtilities`) | AWS SDK default chain: EKS IRSA, Pod Identity, instance profile. Region from `AWS_REGION` or `DB_AWS_REGION` |
| `azure-entra` | Entra access token for scope `DB_AZURE_SCOPE` (default `https://ossrdbms-aad.database.windows.net/.default`) | `DefaultAzureCredential`: AKS Workload Identity, managed identity, `az login` |
| `gcp-iam` | OAuth2 access token for scope `DB_GCP_SCOPE` (default `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/sqlservice.login`) used as the password | Application Default Credentials: GKE Workload Identity, metadata server |

Rules and behaviour:

- `POSTGRES_USER` is the database role mapped to the cloud identity: an RDS
  user granted `rds_iam`, an Entra principal created as a role, or the Cloud
  SQL IAM user (for a service account its email without `.gserviceaccount.com`).
  `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` is not needed and is ignored (a warning is logged if set).
- **TLS is mandatory.** Core refuses to start unless `spring.datasource.url`
  would make the pgjdbc driver encrypt: `sslmode=require`, `verify-ca` or
  `verify-full`, or `ssl=true` (which pgjdbc treats as `verify-full`). The check
  reads the URL the way the driver does (parameter names are case-sensitive, so
  `SSLMODE=...` does not count; the last occurrence wins) and a test compares it
  with the real driver. With Helm set `global.postgresql.sslMode`; otherwise set
  `SPRING_DATASOURCE_URL` yourself, for example
  `jdbc:postgresql://<host>:5432/<db>?options=-c%20TimeZone%3DUTC&sslmode=verify-full&sslrootcert=/certs/ca.pem`.
  Be aware of what each value gives you: `require` encrypts but does **not**
  authenticate the server, so an active man in the middle could capture the
  token (valid for minutes). `verify-ca` / `verify-full` authenticate it but need
  the provider's CA where the driver can find it (`sslrootcert=<file>`, or
  pgjdbc's default `~/.postgresql/root.crt`); the RDS, Azure and Cloud SQL CAs
  are not in that default location, and Cloud SQL server certificates do not
  match the instance host name (`verify-ca` at most, or the Cloud SQL
  connector). The Helm chart only emits `sslmode`: it has no knob to mount a CA
  file, so supplying one means overriding `SPRING_DATASOURCE_URL` and mounting
  the file yourself. This CA handling has not been exercised against any real
  provider.
- A token is cached and refreshed `DB_TOKEN_REFRESH_SKEW` (default `2m`) before
  it expires. If a refresh fails while the cached token is still valid it is
  reused, a warning is logged, and the source is not asked again for 30 seconds
  (so an identity-provider outage does not make every new connection wait on a
  failing call); after the token has expired, opening a connection fails with
  SQLSTATE `28000`. Already-open pooled connections are not re-authenticated,
  so they keep working until the server or Hikari (`maxLifetime`) closes them.
- The token modes connect core straight to the managed server. Do not put
  PgBouncer in front of them; the managed offerings have their own pooler
  (RDS Proxy, Azure built-in PgBouncer, Cloud SQL managed connection pooling),
  which must still be in session mode.
- An unknown `DB_AUTH` value fails startup. In `password` mode a missing or
  blank `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` fails startup with a clear message (it used to fail
  on the unresolved placeholder). This is enforced in code and tested with
  Spring Boot's `DataSourceAutoConfiguration` loaded; it is skipped only when a
  `JdbcConnectionDetails` bean other than Boot's own properties-backed one
  supplies the login (Testcontainers `@ServiceConnection`).
- **Compose:** the shipped `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml` routes core
  through PgBouncer (`DB_HOST: pgbouncer`) and requires
  `POSTGRES_APP_PASSWORD`, and it sets no `DB_AUTH`. The token modes are
  therefore not wired into the shipped Compose files; they target a managed
  database from Kubernetes (or a Compose file of your own that points core at
  the managed server directly).
- **AWS credentials:** IRSA (web identity) needs the AWS SDK `sts` module, which
  core ships (a test fails if it disappears); EKS Pod Identity and instance
  profiles need nothing extra.
- Helm: `global.postgresql.auth` sets it, requires an external `host` and a
  `sslMode` of `require`, `verify-ca` or `verify-full`, and drops the password
  Secret from the core pods. Put the cloud identity on the ServiceAccounts of
  `core`, `core-jobs` and `core-worker` through `<chart>.serviceAccount.annotations`:
  `eks.amazonaws.com/role-arn` (IRSA), `azure.workload.identity/client-id`
  (plus the pod label `azure.workload.identity/use: "true"`), or
  `iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account`. Keycloak's own database is separate and
  still logs in with a password.
- Helm NetworkPolicies (on by default) block the paths a token login needs, so
  with a token mode the chart adds egress rules for the core pods **and the
  migrate Job**: HTTPS (443) to public addresses (AWS STS, Entra), plus one /32
  rule for the credential endpoint that is link-local: `169.254.170.23:80`
  (EKS Pod Identity agent) for `aws-iam`, `169.254.169.254:80` (GKE metadata
  server) for `gcp-iam`. The node's own metadata service is deliberately not
  opened on AWS or Azure (it would hand the node role to any pod). Not covered,
  add them with `global.networkPolicy.extraEgress` /
  `global.networkPolicy.coreEgress.cidrs` if you need them: STS or Entra reached
  through a VPC / private endpoint (private address), Pod Identity over IPv6,
  GKE Dataplane V1 / Calico metadata ports (`169.254.169.252:988`), and a
  database reached through a private address that is not covered by
  `global.postgresql.port` (the database port itself is opened automatically).
  The rendered rules are checked by `deploy/helm/test-guards.sh`; that they are
  enough on a real cluster has not been tried.
- `DB_AWS_REGION` (blank = SDK chain), `DB_AZURE_SCOPE` and `DB_GCP_SCOPE`
  exist for sovereign clouds and unusual setups; leave them unset otherwise.

Status: the caching logic, the TLS guard (compared with the real pgjdbc
parser), the startup check and the wiring (with Spring Boot's
`DataSourceAutoConfiguration` loaded, through the pool, with the default AWS
token path) are unit-tested; the AWS token is produced by the real SDK signer
offline; the Helm guards and NetworkPolicy rules are rendered and asserted by
`deploy/helm/test-guards.sh`. The Azure and GCP token sources only wrap their
SDKs and have no test. Flyway and JPA are not started in these tests (they take
"the" `DataSource` by type; the test proves that is the token pool). No run
against a real RDS, Azure or Cloud SQL instance, with real workload-identity
credentials, or on a cluster with the NetworkPolicies enforced has been done.
All three cloud SDKs are on the classpath of every core shape, including
password-mode images.

## Authentication (OIDC / Keycloak)

This section is the Keycloak (`app.auth.mode=keycloak`, default) setup. For the single-owner alternative that needs no identity server, see [Local sign-in](#local-sign-in-appauthmodelocal).

| Property | Env | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `spring.security.oauth2.resourceserver.jwt.issuer-uri` | `KEYCLOAK_ISSUER_URI` | none: required under the `security` profile when `app.auth.mode=keycloak` (the default); core refuses to start without it, with a message naming this variable (never a `localhost` default). Compose and Helm derive it from `PUBLIC_AUTH_URL` (`<PUBLIC_AUTH_URL>/realms/<KEYCLOAK_REALM>`). With `app.auth.mode=local` it is not needed; the `personal` profile fills in a URN that names no issuer |
| `app.keycloak.jwk-set-uri` | `KEYCLOAK_JWK_SET_URI` | `<issuer>/protocol/openid-connect/certs` |
| `app.keycloak.additional-issuers` | `KEYCLOAK_ADDITIONAL_ISSUERS` | empty; comma-separated. Compose also passes it to code-intel, app-builder and rl-trainer (`CODE_INTEL_`/`APP_BUILDER_`/`RL_TRAINER_KEYCLOAK_ADDITIONAL_ISSUERS`), which validate `iss` against the same list; their `*_KEYCLOAK_ISSUER` has no default and must be set under `*_AUTH_MODE=keycloak` |
| `app.keycloak.require-email-verification` | `KEYCLOAK_REQUIRE_EMAIL_VERIFICATION` (lite) | `true`; the lite Compose tier sets `false` because it has no SMTP. A new user gets the `VERIFY_EMAIL` required action only when true; with no mail path that action ends the first sign-up on "Failed to send email". |
| `app.keycloak.admin-base-url`, `app.keycloak.realm` | `KEYCLOAK_ADMIN_BASE_URL`, `KEYCLOAK_REALM` | `http://localhost:8180`, `agentic-ai` |
| `app.keycloak.client-id` / `client-secret` | `KEYCLOAK_CLIENT_ID` / `KEYCLOAK_CLIENT_SECRET` | `agentic-ai-core` |
| `app.security.oidc.roles-claim-path` | `OIDC_ROLES_CLAIM_PATH` | `realm_access.roles` — dot-path or literal claim name carrying role/authority strings; a flat namespaced key works too (e.g. `https://example.com/roles` for Auth0) |
| `app.security.oidc.tenant-claim` | `OIDC_TENANT_CLAIM` | `tenant_id` — the token claim that resolves the tenant; a missing claim fails closed |
| `app.security.oidc.scopes-claim` | `OIDC_SCOPES_CLAIM` | empty (off) — claim carrying OAuth scopes as a space-delimited string or list; emitted as additional `SCOPE_*` authorities only, never a substitute for stored `PERM_*` grants |
| `app.identity.provider` | `IDENTITY_PROVIDER` | `keycloak` — selects the `IdentityUserAdminPort` bean: `keycloak` → `KeycloakAdminClient`, `auth0` → `Auth0UserAdminClient`. Exactly one is created; per-tenant SSO (`/api/tenant/sso`, incl. SCIM token minting) and `/api/auth/social-providers` are Keycloak-only and 404 under `auth0` |
| `app.identity.auth0.domain` | `AUTH0_DOMAIN` | none; required when `IDENTITY_PROVIDER=auth0`. Bare host or `https://` URL of the Auth0 tenant |
| `app.identity.auth0.client-id` / `client-secret` | `AUTH0_CLIENT_ID` / `AUTH0_CLIENT_SECRET` | none; a Machine-to-Machine application authorized against the Management API. Scopes: `read:users`, `create:users`, `update:users`, `delete:users`, `read:roles`, `delete:sessions`, `delete:refresh_tokens`; it must also have `AUTH0_CONNECTION` enabled (Auth0's emailed password reset refuses unlinked clients) |
| `app.identity.auth0.connection` | `AUTH0_CONNECTION` | `Username-Password-Authentication` — the database connection users are created on |
| `app.identity.auth0.audience` | `AUTH0_AUDIENCE` | `https://<AUTH0_DOMAIN>/api/v2/` — Management API audience for the client_credentials grant |

Non-Keycloak issuers (Auth0, Microsoft Entra, Okta): set `KEYCLOAK_ISSUER_URI`
and `KEYCLOAK_JWK_SET_URI` to the provider's endpoints (`jwk-set-uri` has a
Keycloak-style default), map claims with the `app.security.oidc.*` properties
above, and set `APP_KEYCLOAK_ALLOWED_AZP` (its default lists Keycloak's
first-party client names) or empty it to disable the azp check. For Auth0,
also set `IDENTITY_PROVIDER=auth0` and the `AUTH0_*` variables so the
user-admin surface works — without them the console can validate Auth0
tokens but cannot list, create or delete users. Auth0 users must carry
`app_metadata.tenant_id` and a roles claim mapped via `OIDC_ROLES_CLAIM_PATH`
(e.g. a namespaced `https://<ns>/roles` emitted by an Auth0 Action).

**Keycloak itself (Compose).** Full runs Keycloak in production mode
(`start`) on its own PostgreSQL; the domain overlay always does, and
`KEYCLOAK_START_COMMAND=start-dev` is refused there. Lite uses `start-dev`
with an embedded store, for a single machine only. The realm import
(`deploy/keycloak/agentic-ai-realm.json`, first start only) has no fallback
client secret (`KEYCLOAK_CLIENT_SECRET` is required), redirect URIs and web
origins only from `PUBLIC_APP_URL`/`PUBLIC_ADMIN_URL`, PKCE S256 on every
public client, and the password grant off on the core client
(`KC_CORE_DIRECT_GRANTS=true` turns it on, for CI logins only). core's service
account gets `view-users`, `query-users`, `manage-users`, `query-groups`,
`view-realm`, `view-identity-providers` and `manage-identity-providers` of
`realm-management`, not `realm-admin`; `scripts/vegaduta up` removes a
`realm-admin` grant an older install made. Sign-up e-mail verification follows
`KC_VERIFY_EMAIL` (off by default because it needs SMTP; `doctor` warns on a
domain install).

**Dual issuer.** The `iss` claim in a token is the URL the *browser* used to
reach Keycloak (for example `https://auth.example.com/realms/agentic-ai`), while
core often fetches keys over an internal address (`http://keycloak:8080`). Core
accepts the primary issuer plus every entry in `KEYCLOAK_ADDITIONAL_ISSUERS`.
If the public auth URL is in neither, **every request fails with 401**. Keep
the primary issuer and add the public one; don't replace the primary. The
JWK URL may stay internal.

**Browser URLs.** The consoles read `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_BASE_URL`,
`NEXT_PUBLIC_KEYCLOAK_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_KEYCLOAK_REALM` and
`NEXT_PUBLIC_KEYCLOAK_CLIENT_ID` at runtime, with the value baked in at build
time as fallback. Compose derives them from `PUBLIC_API_URL`, `PUBLIC_AUTH_URL`
and `PUBLIC_APP_URL`. On a server, none of these may be `localhost`. Core's
CORS list is `app.web.allowed-origins` (`APP_WEB_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`), which must
contain the console origins.

| Property | Env | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `app.web.public-url` | `APP_WEB_PUBLIC_URL` (Compose sets it from `PUBLIC_APP_URL`; `PUBLIC_APP_URL` alone also works) | none. The one browser-facing origin of the tenant console that share links, e-mail links and OAuth console redirects are built on. Blank fails start-up under the `security` profile and logs a warning otherwise; there is no `localhost` default |
| `app.google.oauth.console-redirect-uri` | `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CONSOLE_REDIRECT_URI` | blank = `<app.web.public-url>/admin/integrations` |
| `app.x.oauth.console-redirect-uri` | `X_OAUTH_CONSOLE_REDIRECT_URI` | blank = `<app.web.public-url>/admin/channels` |
| `app.atlassian.oauth.console-redirect-uri` | `ATLASSIAN_OAUTH_CONSOLE_REDIRECT_URI` | blank = `<app.web.public-url>/tools` |
| `app.newsletter.unsubscribe-mailto` | (property only) | blank = the mail sender address (`spring.mail.username`). The `mailto:` target of the `List-Unsubscribe` header on platform-admin newsletters |

## Encryption key management (cloud KMS)

`app.crypto.kms.provider` chooses the key that wraps the per-secret data keys of **envelope encryption**
(`EnvelopeEncryptionService`). Today that path protects the payment mandate signing keys only. **About 41 other
classes (provider keys, channel secrets, MCP env) still use `EncryptionService` and the static `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY`;
they are not moved to a KMS yet.** `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` therefore stays required in every case.

| Property | Env | Default / notes |
|---|---|---|
| `app.crypto.kms.provider` | `APP_CRYPTO_KMS_PROVIDER` | `local` (default, unchanged behaviour: `APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY` wraps the data keys) \| `aws` \| `azure` \| `gcp`. Case-insensitive; an empty value means `local`. Anything else, or a cloud value without a key id, fails startup |
| `app.crypto.kms.aws.key-id` / `region` | `APP_CRYPTO_KMS_AWS_KEY_ID` / `APP_CRYPTO_KMS_AWS_REGION` | key ARN (preferred; an alias works but is not stable: re-pointing it makes `Decrypt` fail, and the provider id is the string you configured, so swapping alias and ARN reads as a different key); region blank = SDK default chain. Credentials: AWS default chain (IRSA / Pod Identity / instance role) |
| `app.crypto.kms.azure.key-id` / `algorithm` | `APP_CRYPTO_KMS_AZURE_KEY_ID` / `APP_CRYPTO_KMS_AZURE_ALGORITHM` | key URL `https://<vault>.vault.azure.net/keys/<name>` (no version = newest wraps; a pinned version wraps with that version and gives the same provider id; the version and the wrap algorithm used are stored with each wrapped key, so rotating or changing the algorithm does not strand data); algorithm default `RSA-OAEP-256`, must be a known Key Vault wrap algorithm or startup fails. Credentials: `DefaultAzureCredential` (workload identity, managed identity) |
| `app.crypto.kms.gcp.key-name` / `endpoint` | `APP_CRYPTO_KMS_GCP_KEY_NAME` / `APP_CRYPTO_KMS_GCP_ENDPOINT` | `projects/P/locations/L/keyRings/R/cryptoKeys/K` (symmetric, no version); endpoint default `https://cloudkms.googleapis.com`, must be `https` (the token and data key go to it). Credentials: Application Default Credentials (GKE Workload Identity) |
| `app.crypto.kms.gcp.allow-insecure-test-endpoint` | `APP_CRYPTO_KMS_GCP_ALLOW_INSECURE_TEST_ENDPOINT` | `false`. Only for tests against a local fake: lets `endpoint` be plain `http` to `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`[::1]`. Never set it in a deployment |

Permissions the workload identity needs (on that one key):

| Cloud | Needed |
|---|---|
| AWS | `kms:GenerateDataKey` and `kms:Decrypt` (the calls carry the encryption context `purpose=vegaduta-envelope`) |
| Azure | key permissions `wrapKey` and `unwrapKey` (RBAC role "Key Vault Crypto User"); an RSA key |
| GCP | `cloudkms.cryptoKeyVersions.useToEncrypt` and `useToDecrypt` (role "Cloud KMS CryptoKey Encrypter/Decrypter") |

Behaviour to know:

- Every envelope records the provider id (`aws:<key id>`, `azure:<key url>`, `gcp:<key name>`, or `local`). Decrypting
  an envelope whose provider/key is not registered fails with a message naming both ids; a wrong key is never tried.
- Switching `local` to a cloud provider is safe for existing data: the local provider stays registered, so envelopes
  written before the switch keep decrypting and new ones use the cloud key. Switching **away** from a cloud provider,
  or changing the key id, strands what that key wrapped. Rotate inside the KMS (AWS and GCP key rotation keep old
  versions usable) rather than by changing the id.
- Set the same values on every core shape (api, jobs, worker).
- Startup versus first use differs by cloud. GCP resolves Application Default Credentials in the constructor, so
  missing credentials fail boot. AWS builds its client at startup but resolves credentials on the first call, so a
  bad role shows up on the first wrap or unwrap. Azure builds each `CryptographyClient` (one per key identifier,
  sharing one credential and HTTP client) on first use, so a bad identity or vault URL also shows up there.
- Availability: the payment signing key is decrypted on every mandate signing, so under a cloud provider each
  signature makes one KMS / Key Vault / Cloud KMS call. Their availability, latency and quotas are on that path.
- Integrity binding differs: AWS binds the encryption context and GCP binds additional authenticated data
  (`purpose=vegaduta-envelope`); Azure `wrapKey` has no such parameter. GCP responses are not CRC32C-checked.
- Helm: `global.core.extraEnv` (for example `APP_CRYPTO_KMS_PROVIDER: aws`), plus the cloud's workload identity
  annotations through `serviceAccount.annotations` of the `core`, `core-jobs` and `core-worker` charts. That wiring is
  the existing chart mechanism; it was not rendered or run with these settings.

## File storage

| Property | Env | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `app.storage.type` | `APP_STORAGE_TYPE` | `s3` (Lite profile: `local`) |
| `app.storage.local.path` | `APP_STORAGE_LOCAL_PATH` | `/data/storage` in Lite; required when `type=local` |
| `app.storage.endpoint` | `STORAGE_ENDPOINT` | `http://localhost:9000` (Full: SeaweedFS) |
| `app.storage.region` / `bucket` | `STORAGE_REGION` / `STORAGE_BUCKET` | `us-east-1` / `agentic-ai` |
| `app.storage.access-key` / `secret-key` | `STORAGE_ACCESS_KEY` / `STORAGE_SECRET_KEY` | set real values; the built-in defaults are for local development only. Read only with `type=s3` and `credentials=static` (the MinIO-default WARN is skipped otherwise) |
| `spring.data.redis.ssl.enabled` / `username` | `REDIS_SSL` / `REDIS_USERNAME` | TLS and ACL user for managed Redis-protocol services (ElastiCache, Azure Cache for Redis, Memorystore); defaults keep the plain connection and default user |
| `spring.kafka.security.protocol`, `spring.kafka.properties.sasl.*` | `SPRING_KAFKA_SECURITY_PROTOCOL`, `SPRING_KAFKA_PROPERTIES_SASL_MECHANISM`, `SPRING_KAFKA_PROPERTIES_SASL_JAAS_CONFIG` | managed Kafka (Event Hubs: `SASL_SSL` + `PLAIN`; MSK SCRAM: `SASL_SSL` + `SCRAM-SHA-512`); the JAAS line carries the credential, keep it in a Secret. MSK IAM auth needs a client library that is not in the image |
| `app.storage.type=azure-blob`, `app.storage.azure.endpoint` / `connection-string` | `APP_STORAGE_TYPE`, `STORAGE_AZURE_ENDPOINT`, `STORAGE_AZURE_CONNECTION_STRING` | Azure Blob Storage (Azure has no S3 API); the container is `STORAGE_BUCKET`. With `STORAGE_CREDENTIALS=default` it uses `DefaultAzureCredential` (AKS workload identity, managed identity) against the endpoint and stores no key; with `static` it uses the connection string. **No demo-image expiry on Azure:** S3 gets a bucket lifecycle rule that expires `demo-images/` objects after `app.demo.lifetime-days`; Azure Blob lifecycle management is an account-level policy on the ARM management plane, which the data-plane SDK (and the application identity) cannot set. Create it yourself on the storage account: a `delete` action on base blobs `daysAfterModificationGreaterThan: <lifetime-days>` with prefix `<container>/demo-images/` (`az storage account management-policy create`). Until then those objects are kept. A missing container is an error, as a missing S3 bucket is, not a "not found" |
| `app.storage.credentials` | `STORAGE_CREDENTIALS` | `static` (default: the key pair above) or `default`: the AWS SDK credential chain with no keys (EKS IRSA / Pod Identity, ECS or EC2 role, `AWS_*` env). For AWS S3 (needs the `sts` module for IRSA, which core ships); with `app.storage.type=azure-blob` it selects `DefaultAzureCredential`. GCS needs HMAC keys (`static`). LiveKit call recording uploads with the key pair above, so it needs `type=s3` with `static`; with `default` or `azure-blob` a recording request fails immediately with a message naming this. In Kubernetes only token-file identities (IRSA, Azure workload identity) work under the chart's default NetworkPolicy; see "Cloud identity for object storage" in `deploy/helm/README.md` |
| `app.storage.path-style-access` | `STORAGE_PATH_STYLE_ACCESS` | `true` |

`local` works for a single core process. Anything with more than one core
replica (Full with `jobs`, Kubernetes) needs S3.

## Embeddings and knowledge

| Property | Env | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `app.embeddings.provider` | `APP_EMBEDDINGS_PROVIDER` | `sidecar`; or `openai-compatible`; or `ollama` (free local embeddings; a private or loopback Ollama host must be listed in `APP_SECURITY_OUTBOUND_ALLOWED_PRIVATE_HOSTS`, e.g. `ollama:11434`); or `auto`: a cloud key (`app.embeddings.api-key` with `base-url`) wins, else a configured Ollama (`app.embeddings.ollama.base-url`, or the local-LLM bootstrap Ollama), else an Ollama registered at run time (a platform-pool row pointing at the model server the local-model wizard manages; read per call, cached 30 s), else the sidecar |
| `app.embeddings.base-url` / `api-key` / `model` | `APP_EMBEDDINGS_BASE_URL` / `APP_EMBEDDINGS_API_KEY` / `APP_EMBEDDINGS_MODEL` | for `openai-compatible` (OpenAI, Ollama, vLLM, LiteLLM, ...) |
| `app.embeddings.dimensions` | `APP_EMBEDDINGS_DIMENSIONS` | `384` (must be 1..384) |
| `app.sidecar.base-url` | `SIDECAR_BASE_URL` | `http://localhost:8001`; extraction, transcription, reranking |
| `app.sidecar.enabled` | `APP_SIDECAR_ENABLED` | `true`. `false` makes the sidecar optional: no boot dependency, rerank fails open, `html`/`htm`/`docx` are extracted in process, and PDF/OCR, transcription, TTS/captions/render and sidecar embeddings answer `503` naming this setting. The health surfaces report it as not configured, not down |
| `app.embeddings.ollama.base-url` | `APP_EMBEDDINGS_OLLAMA_BASE_URL` | blank: falls back to the local-LLM bootstrap Ollama (`app.local-llm.bootstrap.base-url`) |
| `app.embeddings.ollama.model` | `APP_EMBEDDINGS_OLLAMA_MODEL` | `nomic-embed-text`. Known: `nomic-embed-text` (768), `mxbai-embed-large` (1024), `bge-m3` (1024), `all-minilm` (384), `snowflake-arctic-embed`, `qwen3-embedding`, `embeddinggemma` (768). An unknown model's width is learned from its first response |
| `app.embeddings.ollama.dimensions` | `APP_EMBEDDINGS_OLLAMA_DIMENSIONS` | `0`: take the width from the model table, else learn it |
| `app.embeddings.ollama.allow-truncation` | (none) | unset: follow the model table. `true` allows cutting and re-normalising a vector wider than the pgvector column (768 knowledge, 384 memory and cache) for a model that is not Matryoshka-trained |
| `app.embeddings.ollama.query-prefix` / `document-prefix` | (none) | unset: the model's default prefix; an empty string means none |
| `app.embeddings.ollama.keep-alive` | `APP_EMBEDDINGS_OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` | `10m` |
| `app.knowledge.embedding-binding.enabled` | `KNOWLEDGE_EMBEDDING_BINDING_ENABLED` | `true`. Each knowledge collection records which embedder wrote it (`sidecar` or `provider:model:width`, migration V550). Ingesting with a different one answers `409` naming `POST /api/knowledge/collections/{id}/reembed`, and vector search skips the mismatched collection (keyword search still answers). `false` turns the guard off |
| `app.knowledge.rerank.enabled` | `KNOWLEDGE_RERANK_ENABLED` | `false` |

Changing the embedding provider on a populated database requires re-embedding
every knowledge collection.

## LLM providers and keys

There are **no LLM API keys in core's configuration**. Tenants add provider
keys in the console (bring your own key); they are stored encrypted with
`APP_ENCRYPTION_KEY`. Supported providers are listed in
`core/providers/.../ProviderName.java`.

| Property | Env | Default | |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.agents.default-model` | `DEFAULT_MODEL` | `claude-sonnet-4-5` | model preselected for new agents |
| hosted pool ordering | `HOSTED_POOL_ORDER_BY_COST` | `true` | platform-operated shared pool (optional), configured by a platform admin |
| `app.llm.default-source` | (none) | `auto` | which source a tenant that has made no explicit choice uses: `platform-pool`, `byok`, or `auto` (the pool when one is configured, otherwise the tenant's own key) |
| `app.free-tier.monthly-token-limit` | (none) | `400000` | rolling-30-day token quota per FREE tenant on the pool (PREMIUM ×2, UNLIMITED ×3); editable per deployment in Platform Admin |
| `app.free-tier.meter-zero-cost-rows` | (none) | `false` | see **Zero-cost pool rows** below |
| Vertex AI pool | `VERTEX_ENABLED`, `VERTEX_POOL_*` | `false` | |
| `app.platform-llm.warm.enabled` | `APP_PLATFORM_LLM_WARM_ENABLED` | `true` | when someone signs in or opens a chat, the console calls `POST /api/platform-llm/warm`, and core sends the pool's top row a one-token request so a model that scaled to zero is loaded before the first message |
| `app.platform-llm.warm.interval-seconds` | `APP_PLATFORM_LLM_WARM_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | `240` | at most one warm-up per core instance in this window, however many people sign in |

### Zero-cost pool rows

A pool row the operator runs themselves (the Ollama container that `--local-ai` starts, a vLLM or
llama.cpp server on the LAN, any OpenAI-compatible endpoint nobody bills per token) is marked
**Free (self-hosted)** in Platform Admin (`zero_cost`, migration V484). Such a row is priced at
$0 in `llm_call_log` and in the per-user daily dollar budget (an unknown model id would otherwise be
charged at the conservative fallback rate of $0.50 in / $2.00 out per MTok), is tried first in the
failover order, and when **every** enabled row is zero-cost the monthly token quota is not applied
either: a self-hosted install never shows "Free plan monthly token limit reached". Set
`app.free-tier.meter-zero-cost-rows=true` to keep the token quota for such a pool (one small shared
GPU still needs a ceiling). The bootstrap below creates its row as zero-cost; a mixed pool (paid and
free rows) still reserves tokens and dollars up front and refunds the zero-cost turns at the end.

`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is read only by the evaluation runner (`core/quality`, `eval-harness.md`) and by
optional services (app builder, RL trainer), not by core's chat path.

## Feature flags for optional services

| Env | Default | Enables | Also set |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CODE_INTEL_ENABLED` | `false` | code intelligence (`services/code-intel`) | `CODE_INTEL_BASE_URL`, `CODE_INTEL_INTERNAL_TOKEN` |
| `TOOL_SANDBOX_ENABLED` | `false` | custom Java/Python tools in `services/tool-sandbox` | `TOOL_SANDBOX_BASE_URL`, `TOOL_SANDBOX_INTERNAL_TOKEN` |
| `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_ENABLED` | `false` | run stdio MCP servers (tenant-registered and the `mcp` profile's built-ins) in `services/mcp-stdio-runner` instead of inside core | `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_URL`, `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_SHARED_SECRET` |
| `MCP_STDIO_PREFLIGHT_FAIL_FAST` | `true` | stop core at startup when a stdio launcher it needs is missing (`npx` for the `mcp` profile without the runner, `node` for the runner relay); `false` only logs a warning | |
| `WORKFLOW_EVENTS_ENABLED` | `false` (`true` with the `workflow` profile) | workflow events on Kafka | `WORKFLOW_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS` |
| `WORKFLOW_KAFKA_ENABLED` | unset: `true` if a bootstrap server resolved, else `false` | `false` keeps the `workflow` profile but starts no Kafka topic admin, outbox relay or consumers, and makes `WORKFLOW_EVENTS_ENABLED` default to `false` (an explicit value still wins). When unset, core decides at start-up: Kafka is on exactly when `spring.kafka.bootstrap-servers` is non-blank, whatever supplied it (`WORKFLOW_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS`, `SPRING_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS`, a key in the app Secret, `extraEnvFromSecrets`, `envFrom`, ...); with none it starts no Kafka client and logs one INFO line. The Helm chart never sets this flag (it cannot read Secret contents), so a Secret-held bootstrap is honoured. Only the literal `false` turns it off. The one-shot migrate process always runs with it off. There is no longer an implicit `localhost:9092` default: set `WORKFLOW_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS` (Compose and `core/.env.example` do). | `WORKFLOW_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS` |
| `KNOWLEDGE_RERANK_ENABLED` | `false` | reranking via the sidecar | |
| `GRAPH_MEMORY_ARBITRATION_ENABLED` | `false` | graph-memory conflict arbitration | |
| `APP_A2A_SIGNING_ENABLED` | `true` | Ed25519-signed A2A agent cards | |
| `TOOL_AUTO_SELECTION_ENABLED` | `true` | automatic tool selection per turn | |
| `APP_VISION_ENABLED` | `true` | image inputs in chat | |
| `AGENT_JOB_TRIGGERS_ENABLED` | `true` | scheduled agent jobs | |
| `SIGNUP_DISPOSABLE_EMAIL_BLOCK_ENABLED` | `true` | block disposable email domains at signup | |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_BILLING_ENABLED` (web; Compose: `WEB_BILLING_ENABLED`) | off | plans, prices, upgrade prompts and billing pages; off redirects `/billing`, `/features` and `/admin/billing` and hides every upgrade CTA | `1` to turn on (runtime, see `web/docs/RUNTIME-CONFIG.md`) |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_MODE` (web; Compose: `WEB_SITE_MODE`) | `oss` | which signed-out pages are served: `oss` is the technical open-source landing and About, and redirects the pricing, enterprise, hire, earn, compare and similar sales pages to `/`; `classic` restores the original marketing pages (tag `backup/classic-marketing-pages-2026-10-12`) | `oss` or `classic` (runtime) |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_DOCS_URL` (web; Compose: `WEB_DOCS_URL`) | empty | absolute URL of the published documentation site; when set, the public pages' "Docs" links and the in-app help banner point there, otherwise at the in-app `/docs` help ([site modes](site-modes.md)) | a URL (runtime) |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_ACADEMY_URL` (web) | `https://academy.vegaduta.ai` | origin of the separate (proprietary) Academy learning portal; compose's `WEB_ACADEMY_URL` defaults to the hosted Academy — point it at a self-hosted Academy, or empty in a fork that does not want the links | a URL (runtime) |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_CONTACT_EMAIL` (web; Compose: `WEB_CONTACT_EMAIL`; Helm: `web.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_CONTACT_EMAIL`) | empty | contact address on `/plugins` and `/account-deletion`. Empty shows a placeholder; set an address you own | an e-mail address (runtime) |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_CODE_INTEL_URL` (web; Compose: `PUBLIC_CODE_INTEL_URL`) | empty | browser-facing code-intel URL. Leave empty so the web app uses the core proxy; a `localhost` value is baked into the browser bundle and fails for every non-local viewer. Helm sets it from `global.hosts.codeintel` when the code-intel component is on | a public URL, or empty (runtime) |
| `NEXT_PUBLIC_COMMERCIAL_URL` (web; Compose: `WEB_COMMERCIAL_URL`) | empty | absolute URL of a hosted/commercial offering; when set, the open-source pages show a "Hosted version" link to it, otherwise they show none | a URL (runtime) |

The Compose profiles `code-intel`, `sandbox` and `events` start the services
and the CLI sets these flags for you.

## Agent and platform feature flags

Flags added by the "six for everyone" feature set and the agent-trust,
reliability and evaluation work. Each is a Spring property (`app.*`, shown as
its env name). Defaults are in `application.yml`; the comment above each key
says what it does and what it costs. The nine flags marked **compose** are
passed through by `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml` (set them in
`deploy/compose/.env`); for the rest set the env var on the core container.

**Ship dark (default `false`):**

| Env | Enables | Needs |
|---|---|---|
| `APP_TUTOR_ENABLED` **compose** | Learn Anything tutor: study plans, graded quizzes, spaced-repetition review (`/api/tutor`, `/learn/tutor`). Limits: `APP_TUTOR_MAX_PLANS_PER_USER`, `APP_TUTOR_MAX_LESSONS_PER_PLAN`, `APP_TUTOR_MAX_CURRICULUM_BYTES`, `APP_TUTOR_GENERATE_MAX_PER_USER_PER_HOUR` | |
| `APP_TUTOR_REMINDERS_ENABLED` **compose** | hourly in-app "reviews due" notification (no model call); `APP_TUTOR_REMINDERS_MIN_GAP_HOURS` | tutor on |
| `RESEARCH_DEEP_ENABLED` **compose** | `deep_research` and `verify_citations` tools (bounded search + fetch + quote verification); `RESEARCH_DEEP_*` tune searches, fetches, sources, workers and deadline | a web-search API key (otherwise an honest "unavailable" state) |
| `RESEARCH_RUNS_ENABLED` **compose** | background research runs (`/api/research/runs`, `/research`); `RESEARCH_RUNS_MAX_PER_USER_PER_DAY`, `RESEARCH_RUNS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `RESEARCH_DEEP_ENABLED` |
| `APP_SANDBOX_DATA_ANALYSIS_ENABLED` **compose** | upload CSV/XLSX/JSON files to the `run_code` sandbox; `APP_SANDBOX_DATA_ANALYSIS_MAX_*` and `_TTL_MINUTES` bound files, bytes, sessions | an E2B account and code-execution template |
| `APP_SANDBOX_PLATFORM_E2B_ENABLED` **compose** | let tenants without their own E2B key use a platform-owned one, with daily run caps (`APP_SANDBOX_PLATFORM_E2B_FREE_DAILY_RUNS`, `_PAID_DAILY_RUNS`, `_GLOBAL_DAILY_RUNS`, `_MAX_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`) | platform E2B credential |
| `E2B_RESTRICT_PUBLIC_TRAFFIC` | `true` (default): every E2B sandbox is created with public traffic to its URLs restricted and core sends the `e2b-traffic-access-token` header on every call; a create that returns no token is killed and fails closed. Set `false` only to roll back to open sandbox URLs | an E2B account |
| `WHATSAPP_TEMPLATES_ENABLED` **compose** | send and list approved Meta WhatsApp templates (workflow Output node) | Meta WhatsApp Cloud API connection with a WABA id |
| `WORKFLOW_DRAFT_SIMULATE_SENDS` **compose** | draft workflow runs log "DRY RUN - would ..." for emails, messages and non-GET HTTP calls instead of sending | |
| `GRAPH_MEMORY_USER_FORGET_SYNC_ENABLED` **compose** | deleting a remembered fact also invalidates its graph edges | graph-memory profile; never exercised against real AGE |
| `OPENAI_COMPAT_ENABLED` | inbound OpenAI-compatible API (`POST /v1/chat/completions`, `GET /v1/models`) driving a platform agent; `OPENAI_COMPAT_RATE_LIMIT_*`, `_MAX_PROMPT_CHARS`, `_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, `_FIRST_TOKEN_WAIT_SECONDS` | JWT or `vmcp_` grant key |
| `APP_A2A_CLIENT_ENABLED` | outbound A2A client (call remote agents); `APP_A2A_CLIENT_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is the egress allow-list, `APP_A2A_CLIENT_REQUIRE_SIGNED_CARD` stays `true`; `APP_A2A_CLIENT_MAX_CALLS_PER_TURN` (default 5, 0 = no cap) bounds remote calls per agent turn; `APP_A2A_TASK_STORE=redis` for more than one replica | allow-listed hosts |
| `APP_MCPSERVER_ASYNC_TASKS_ENABLED` | MCP async tasks on the platform MCP server | |
| `MCP_OAUTH_ENABLED` | generic MCP OAuth (RFC 9728/8414/7591 + PKCE), API only; see `docs/architecture/ADR-mcp-generic-oauth.md`. `MCP_OAUTH_STORE` (`memory`/`redis`, default `memory`) chooses where the single-use state set, the token cache and the refresh single-flight live — `redis` is required when the API runs more than one replica (replica-strict boot refuses otherwise; the Helm chart sets it via `core.replicaReadiness.mcpOAuthStoreRedis`) | `MCP_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI` |
| `APP_MCP_REGISTRY_ROUTE_TOOL_CALLS` | route stdio MCP tool calls to the owning replica over Redis | Redis with auth/TLS |
| `AGENT_JOB_STEP_JOURNAL_ENABLED` | durable step journal for resumable agent jobs (`AGENT_JOB_RESUME_FROM_JOURNAL`, `AGENT_JOB_RESUME_RERUN_INCOMPLETE_TURN`, `AGENT_JOB_STEP_JOURNAL_RETENTION_DAYS`) | |
| `CHAT_ESCALATION_ENABLED` | verifier-escalated model routing; `CHAT_ESCALATION_KILL_SWITCH` wins | |
| `SWARM_VERIFIER_ENABLED` | goal verifier for `delegate_parallel` swarms | |
| `CODING_HARNESS_ENABLED` | limits for the coding-agent harness (stage 0, nothing reads them yet) | |
| `CODING_HARNESS_LOCAL_WORKSPACE_DIR` | owner-only parent directory for the dev-only `local` runner's workspaces (default `~/.vegaduta/coding-sandbox`; never the shared system temp dir) | `CODING_HARNESS_ALLOW_LOCAL_RUNNER` |
| `MCP_PACKAGE_CACHE_DIR` | owner-only base directory for the per-registration npm/uv caches of stdio MCP servers spawned inside core (`npm-cache/`, `uv-cache/`, `uv-tools/`, `uv-bin/` sub-roots, swept hourly for orphans; default `~/.vegaduta/mcp-package-cache`, never the shared system temp root). When the default cannot be created (read-only container home) core warns and uses an owner-verified `vegaduta-mcp-package-cache-<user>` directory under the JVM temp dir; an explicit value that is unusable stops boot | |
| `KNOWLEDGE_SPAN_CITATIONS_ENABLED` | span-level offsets and quote on `search_knowledge_base` hits | |
| `EVAL_JUDGE_CALIBRATION_ENABLED` | judge calibration against human-labelled examples | |
| `LLM_STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_NATIVE_ENABLED` | provider-enforced JSON schemas (OpenAI `json_schema`, Gemini `responseSchema`) | |
| `TOOL_SANDBOX_E2B_EXECUTION` | run custom Python/JS tools in an E2B microVM instead of `tool-sandbox` | `TOOL_SANDBOX_ENABLED`, E2B credential |
| `APP_X402_FACILITATOR_URL` (empty) | x402 settlement; empty keeps payments in verification-only mode | |
| `APP_DELEGATION_ISSUER` (empty) | issuer claim for delegation tokens (defaults to the A2A public base URL) | |

**Default `true` (switch off with `false`):**

| Env | Effect |
|---|---|
| `APP_MEMORY_BLOCKS_ENABLED`, `APP_MEMORY_BLOCKS_ACTIVE_PROJECT_ENABLED`, `APP_MEMORY_SELF_SERVICE_ENABLED` | editable memory blocks, project memory and the `/memory` page; size caps in `APP_MEMORY_*_MAX_*` |
| `APP_CHAT_DIRECTIVES_MEMORY_ENABLED`, `APP_CHAT_DIRECTIVES_MCP_UNTRUSTED_ENABLED`, `APP_CHAT_DIRECTIVES_TOOL_TRUST_ENABLED`, `APP_CHAT_SSE_MEMORY_EVENTS_ENABLED` | prompt directives and SSE events for memory and tool trust |
| `TOOL_CONFIRMATION_GATE` | tools classified as needing confirmation park for human approval; `TOOL_CONFIRMATION_GATE_EXEMPT` lists the exceptions |
| `APP_GUARDRAILS_FAIL_MODE` | `closed` (default) or `open`: what a guardrail does when its own machinery fails (tool-policy lookup throws, the injection judge errors or answers nothing, content moderation throws). `closed` refuses the call, withholds the output or refuses the turn; `open` lets it through with a warning. A violation always blocks. Not configured (no bean, no chat client) is not a failure. |
| `TOOL_UNCLASSIFIED_ACTION` | `allow` (default, counted), `require-approval` or `deny`: what a tool with no classification row gets. The last two stop a new or third-party tool acting until it is classified. |
| `TOOL_EFFECT_JOURNAL_ENABLED` | repeated write/send tool calls within `TOOL_EFFECT_JOURNAL_TTL_MINUTES` return the recorded result (`TOOL_EFFECT_JOURNAL_STORE=redis` to share across replicas) |
| `CHAT_TOOL_RESULT_CLEARING_ENABLED` | old tool results are cleared from the prompt past `CHAT_TOOL_RESULT_CLEARING_THRESHOLD_CHARS`, keeping the last `CHAT_TOOL_RESULT_CLEARING_KEEP_LAST` |
| `LLM_ANTHROPIC_THINKING_ENABLED` | extended-thinking mapping for Anthropic; thinking stays off until `LLM_ANTHROPIC_THINKING_EFFORT` (or a per-call option) sets an effort |
| `EVALS_FREE_PLAN_ENABLED`, `AGENT_LAB_FREE_PLAN_ENABLED` | eval datasets and Agent Lab knockout/consistency are available to tenants on the FREE plan with tighter caps (`false` = restore the premium-only 403) |
| `EVAL_CODE_GRADERS_ENABLED`, `EVAL_RUN_SNAPSHOT_ENABLED`, `EVAL_GATE_PASS_HAT_K_ENABLED`, `SIMULATION_ATTACK_SCENARIOS_ENABLED` | deterministic code graders, run snapshots, the pass^k promotion gate, attack-scenario simulations |

Other new settings: `AGENT_RUN_BUDGET_USD` (default `2.0`, cost cap for delegation
and deep research per run, with `AGENT_RUN_BUDGET_PER_AGENT_OVERRIDE_ENABLED` and
`AGENT_RUN_BUDGET_MAX_PER_AGENT_USD`), `APP_AGENTS_DEFAULT_GUARDRAILS`
(default `jailbreak`, seeded on new agents; empty restores the old behaviour),
`EVAL_DEFAULT_REPEATS` (`3`, repeats per eval case for pass^k).

Web (runtime, see `web/docs/RUNTIME-CONFIG.md`): `NEXT_PUBLIC_MCP_APPS=1` renders
sandboxed MCP Apps (`ui://` resources) in chat; `NEXT_PUBLIC_ARTIFACTS=1` turns
on the chat artifacts side pane (Compose: `WEB_ARTIFACTS`); `NEXT_PUBLIC_TRUSTED_IMAGE_HOSTS`
lists extra hosts model-rendered markdown images may load from (an entry
starting with `.` also matches subdomains); the API and site origins are always
trusted.

### Flags added in the October 2026 sync

Same conventions as above (Spring property shown as its env name, defaults in
`application.yml`).

**Ship dark (default `false`):**

| Env | Enables |
|---|---|
| `AGENT_HOOKS_ENABLED` | declarative agent lifecycle hooks in the tool guard chain and the chat turn |
| `TOOL_ARG_POLICIES_ENABLED` | per-agent tool argument policies, evaluated before a tool runs |
| `TOOL_SEARCH_ENABLED` | on-demand tool loading through `search_tools` / `call_tool` |
| `TOOL_MCP_ANNOTATIONS_ENABLED` | classify MCP tools from their safety annotations |
| `TOOL_HTTP_REQUEST_SAFE_MODE` | `http_request` safe mode: https only, allow-lists, named credentials, gated writes |
| `APP_CONTEXT_COMPACTION_ENABLED` | context budget planner and compaction in the chat turn (`restore_tool_result` stays on with it) |
| `APP_CHAT_RESUMABLE_STREAMS_ENABLED` (Helm: `core.resumableStreamsEnabled`) | chat streams that resume after a drop, sleep, refresh or navigation. `APP_CHAT_RESUMABLE_STREAMS_STORE=redis` (set by the chart for 2+ replicas) has no effect while this is off |
| `APP_CHAT_BRANCHING_ENABLED` (Helm: `core.branchingEnabled`) | keep chat history: each message is stored in Postgres for `app.chat.branching.retention-days` (30) instead of expiring from Redis after 24 h; edit, regenerate and switch between conversation branches. Also powers `/api/chat/threads` (the signed-in user's session list, which the Assistant merges into its thread rail) and the stored-tree fallback of the transcript reload after the 24 h Redis window; those routes answer 404 while off. On by default in `lite.yml`, `docker-compose.yml`, the chart and the `personal` Spring profile (`application-personal.yml`, since 0.1.9; `APP_CHAT_BRANCHING_ENABLED=false` in `deploy/compose/core.env` turns it off there); the core property itself defaults to off |
| `APP_CHAT_DIRECTIVES_ARTIFACTS_ENABLED` | prompt directive for the chat artifacts pane |
| `CHAT_IMPORT_ENABLED`, `CHAT_EXPORT_ENABLED` | import ChatGPT/Claude chats as read-only records; export chats as md/json/txt. Transcript reload, export and branching only ever reach the caller's own conversation: session memory is keyed by tenant + agent + user + client session id, and a session is readable only by the user who started it (see Security Architecture §4.1 in the platform docs) |
| `APP_MCP_APPS_ENABLED` | host MCP Apps (`ui://` resources); the web side also needs `NEXT_PUBLIC_MCP_APPS=1` |
| `APP_MCP_SERVER_EXPOSE_AGENTS_ENABLED` | expose agents and workflows as per-caller MCP tools |
| `MCP_OAUTH_PROVIDERS_ENABLED` | one-click provider OAuth (Google, GitHub, Slack, Notion, Linear, HubSpot, Microsoft, Asana, Zoom); per-provider `MCP_OAUTH_<PROVIDER>_CLIENT_ID` / `_CLIENT_SECRET`, `MCP_OAUTH_PROVIDER_CALLBACK_BASE_URL` and `MCP_OAUTH_PROVIDER_CONSOLE_REDIRECT_URI` (public URLs, never localhost) |
| `MCP_OAUTH_CIMD_ENABLED` | Client ID Metadata Documents for generic remote-MCP OAuth (`MCP_OAUTH_CIMD_CLIENT_ID_URL`) |
| `MCP_PIECE_HOST_ENABLED` | Activepieces piece-* catalog entries; needs the runner image from `deploy/compose/docker-compose.piece-host.yml` and `MCP_STDIO_SIDECAR_ENABLED=true` |
| `BROWSER_V2_ENABLED` | (default on in the `personal` profile, with profiles and takeover; Chromium comes from `CORE_IMAGE_VARIANT=-full`, see [personal mode](personal-mode.md#the-assistants-browser)) browser agent v2 tools with domain allow/deny and vault secret placeholders; `BROWSER_PROFILES_ENABLED` (encrypted saved logins), `BROWSER_RECIPES_ENABLED` (record and replay), `BROWSER_TOTP_ENABLED`, `BROWSER_TAKEOVER_ENABLED` (human takeover) |
| `KNOWLEDGE_PARENT_CHILD_ENABLED`, `KNOWLEDGE_FUSION_ENABLED`, `KNOWLEDGE_QUERY_REWRITE_ENABLED` | parent-child chunking, tunable RRF/MMR fusion, query rewriting |
| `DATA_TABLES_ENABLED` | Data Tables service, REST API, agent tools and the `data_table` workflow node |
| `SCHEDULED_TASKS_ENABLED` | recurring agent prompts (`/scheduled`, `schedule_task` tool) |
| `WORKFLOW_FORM_TRIGGER_ENABLED`, `WORKFLOW_WAIT_NODE_ENABLED`, `WORKFLOW_NODE_ERROR_STRATEGY_ENABLED`, `WORKFLOW_PORTABILITY_ENABLED` | hosted form trigger, Wait node, per-node error strategies, workflow import/export |
| `QUALITY_TRAJECTORY_EVAL_ENABLED` | trajectory evals scored with pass^k |
| `AUDIT_SEAL_ENABLED` | signed, hash-chained audit seals; verify exports offline with `scripts/verify-audit-export.py` |
| `WHATSAPP_VOICE_NOTES_ENABLED` | WhatsApp voice notes in the sender's language (Meta Cloud API) |
| `LLM_STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_ANTHROPIC_TOOL_FORCING_ENABLED`, `LLM_STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_OLLAMA_FORMAT_ENABLED`, `LLM_TOOLS_STRICT_ENABLED` | provider-side structured output and strict tool schemas |
| `AGENT_JOB_OWNERSHIP_ENABLED` | agent-job ownership leases, cross-replica adoption and bounded resume |
| `RESILIENCE_CB_ENABLED`, `RESILIENCE_BULKHEAD_ENABLED` | circuit breakers (sidecar embeddings, E2B, search, Meta Graph) and bulkheads |
| `APP_OBSERVABILITY_TAIL_SAMPLING_ENABLED` | in-process tail sampling of errors and slow agent runs |
| `APP_STARTUP_BACKGROUND_WARMUP_ENABLED`, `APP_STARTUP_PROFILING_ENABLED` | move best-effort warm-ups off the main thread; start-up phase profiling |
| `TENANCY_MIGRATE_SKIP_UP_TO_DATE` | skip already-migrated tenant schemas on boot |

**Default `true`:** `AGENT_IMPORT_ENABLED` (import agents from OpenAI
Assistants, the Agents SDK and Flowise), `APP_MEMORY_IMPORT_ENABLED` (import
memories from another assistant), `SKILLS_SUPPLY_CHAIN_ENABLED` (scan, pin and
review skills), `WORKFLOW_AI_NODES_ENABLED` (question classifier, parameter
extractor, variable aggregator, document extractor), `TOOL_HTTP_REQUEST_ENABLED`
and `TOOL_HTTP_REQUEST_WRITE_TOOL_ENABLED`, `PLATFORM_RELIABILITY_ENABLED` and
`PLATFORM_NORTH_STAR_ENABLED` (admin Reliability and North Star pages), and the
per-provider `MCP_OAUTH_<PROVIDER>_ENABLED` switches (inert while
`MCP_OAUTH_PROVIDERS_ENABLED` is off).

Other new settings:

- `APP_REPLICA_MODE` (`single`): set `multi` when more than one core replica
  shares a database; the startup check then lists every in-memory store that
  would split state across replicas (a warning each). With
  `APP_REPLICA_MODE_STRICT=true` the fatal ones refuse to boot. The Helm chart
  sets both for you when the API runs more than one replica
  ([Deployment shapes](deploy/DEPLOYMENT-SHAPES.md#running-the-api-on-more-than-one-replica)).
- `app.chat.hotpath.agent-projection-ttl` (`3s`, capped at `30s`): how long a pod
  keeps its snapshot of an agent's read-only settings between turns. A write on the
  same pod evicts it at once; another replica sees the change after at most this
  long. `0s` reads the row every time. Authorization never uses it. See
  [Hot-path performance](architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md).
- `app.chat.hotpath.plan-cache-ttl` (`0s`, capped at `30s`): how long a pod keeps the
  tenant's resolved plan between turns. Off by default: a turn now resolves the plan once and
  passes it down, so the cache could save at most one lookup per turn (the measured reasoning is
  in [Hot-path performance](architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md)). A plan change on the same pod
  evicts at once; another replica sees it after at most this long. Never used for the tenant kill
  switch.
- `app.chat.hotpath.config-lookup-ttl` (`3s`, capped at `30s`): how long a pod keeps
  "is this integration configured" answers (any MCP server, the ElevenLabs, Sarvam and E2B
  credentials, image and video providers, payment mandates, specialist tags, platform tool
  credentials). A write on the same pod evicts at once; another replica sees it after at most
  this long. `0s` turns the cache off.
- `app.chat.session-append-single-roundtrip` (`true`): append one conversation turn to
  Redis in one Lua call instead of 3 to 6 sequential commands. Same ordering, TTL and
  trimming; `false` restores the multi-call sequence, which is also the automatic fallback
  if Redis refuses the script before running it (ACL, scripting disabled, Redis Cluster
  cross-slot). A timeout or dropped connection is not replayed, since the script may have run.
- `app.chat.cancel-poll-interval` (`500ms`): how often a pod checks Redis for cancel flags
  of its in-flight chat turns. One Lua call on the shared connection covers every turn on the pod, and nothing is
  sent while none is streaming. Worst case from a cancel request to the turn stopping is this
  interval plus one Redis command.
- `app.chat.stream-end.async` (`false`): run the best-effort work at the end of a chat stream
  (output-guardrail audit, `agent.message.completed` event, RL episode close, LLM call-log row)
  on a small pool instead of the thread that delivered the last token. Off until measured.
  `app.chat.stream-end.threads` (`4`), `app.chat.stream-end.queue` (`1000`; a full queue runs
  the work on the streaming thread, nothing is dropped), `app.chat.stream-end.drain-timeout`
  (`10s`; queued work is run on a graceful stop). A hard kill loses what is still queued. See
  [Hot-path performance](architecture/HOT-PATH-PERFORMANCE.md).
- `app.turn-journal.enabled` (`false`): write a hash-chained journal of each chat turn's stages
  (intake, model call, tool calls, outbound guard, verify, commit) to the tenant table
  `turn_journal_event`. Hashes and short redacted summaries only, never prompts or tool payloads.
  Off, behaviour is unchanged. Read one turn with `GET /api/agents/{agentId}/turns/{turnId}/journal`
  (needs the audit-log permission). See [ADR-0020](architecture/ADR-0020-turn-journal.md).
- A2A signing keys: `APP_A2A_SIGNING_ENABLED` (`true`) signs Agent Cards (Ed25519) and, with the same key,
  delegation tokens and audit seals (`AUDIT_SEAL_ENABLED`). With `APP_A2A_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY`,
  `APP_A2A_SIGNING_PUBLIC_KEY` (PEM) and `APP_A2A_SIGNING_KEY_ID` empty (the default) each boot, and each
  replica, signs with an ephemeral key, so cards verify at `/.well-known/jwks.json` only until restart. Pin all
  three for a stable identity; the Helm chart refuses more than one API replica until
  `core.replicaReadiness.a2aSigningKeysPinned` is `true` (you supply the keys through the app Secret).
- `APP_DELEGATION_MAX_TTL_SECONDS` (`3600`): ceiling on any delegation token's life and on a grant's TTL.
- `app.scheduling.skip-bean-classes`, `app.scheduling.keep-bean-classes`,
  `app.scheduling.skip-listener-classes`: which scheduled beans and boot
  listeners a shape or pool leaves to another. A class name or a package prefix
  ending in `*`; `keep-bean-classes` carves exceptions out of a broad skip.
  Set by the `shape-*` profiles, not by hand.
- `app.scheduling.pool` (`APP_SCHEDULING_POOL`, empty), `app.scheduling.offloaded-pools`
  (`APP_SCHEDULING_OFFLOADED_POOLS`, empty): named pools of the jobs shape, defined in
  `app.scheduling.pools.<name>` (`application-shape-jobs.yml`: `ingest`, `webhooks`, `intake`, `billing`).
  On a pool pod, `pool=<name>` skips every product scheduled bean except that pool's (with the
  `shape-jobs-pool` profile, which also skips the boot sweeps another shape owns). On the main jobs pod,
  `offloaded-pools=<a,b>` skips the classes of pools that run elsewhere. Both empty = the pod runs everything
  its shape owns. An unknown pool name fails the boot. The two are exclusive. Set by the Helm pool charts,
  not by hand.
- `TOOL_TRIFECTA_ENFORCEMENT` (`warn`): lethal-trifecta enforcement for agents
  combining private data, untrusted content and external communication -
  `off`, `warn`, `require-approval` or `block`.
- Web search: `WEB_SEARCH_PROVIDER` (`brave`), `WEB_SEARCH_FALLBACK` (empty),
  per-provider daily caps `WEB_SEARCH_CAP_<PROVIDER>` (`0` = unlimited), and for
  SearXNG `WEB_SEARCH_SEARXNG_BASE_URL`, `_CATEGORIES`, `_ENGINES`, `_LANGUAGE`.
  Tavily, Exa and Serper keys are entered in the admin console. A self-hosted
  SearXNG is in `deploy/compose/docker-compose.search.yml` (profile `search`).
- Billing: `DEFAULT_PAYMENT_GATEWAY` (`razorpay`); Stripe via
  `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`,
  `STRIPE_PRICE_ID_PREMIUM`, `STRIPE_PRICE_ID_UNLIMITED` (blank = Stripe
  reports not configured; an empty webhook secret fails closed).
  `BILLING_EMAIL_UPGRADE_URL` (`billing.email.upgrade-url`, empty): link used by
  the complimentary-plan reminder and "now on Free" e-mails; blank =
  `<app.web.public-url>/billing`.

### Outbound timeouts and tool search

Verified against `application.yml` and `application-workflow.yml`. Spring properties; the env name is the override.

| Property | Environment variable | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.llm.http.connect-timeout` | `LLM_HTTP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` | `10s` | connect timeout to an LLM provider; the one that fires in practice (outage, DNS blackhole, a wrong Ollama base URL) |
| `app.llm.http.read-timeout` | `LLM_HTTP_READ_TIMEOUT` | `10m` | time until the provider starts responding, not a limit on the whole answer: a hang detector |
| `app.llm.http.idle-connection-timeout` | `LLM_HTTP_IDLE_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT` | `120s` | how long an idle pooled provider connection may live; keep it below a NAT idle window (about 4 minutes on Azure). `0s` keeps the JDK default |
| `app.llm.http.stream-timeouts-enabled` | `LLM_HTTP_STREAM_TIMEOUTS_ENABLED` | `true` | escape hatch: `false` hands back the auto-detected streaming connector and gives up streaming-path timeouts |
| `app.llm.http.stream-idle-timeout` | `LLM_HTTP_STREAM_IDLE_TIMEOUT` | `90s` | no new token for this long ends the fast-depth stream with a timeout |
| `app.llm.http.buffered-turn-timeout` | `LLM_HTTP_BUFFERED_TURN_TIMEOUT` | `240s` | total ceiling for one buffered-depth turn (careful, rigorous, planned) |
| `app.http.hcaptcha.connect-timeout` / `read-timeout` | `HCAPTCHA_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` / `HCAPTCHA_READ_TIMEOUT` | `3s` / `5s` | the hCaptcha verification call |
| `spring.mail.properties.mail.smtp.connectiontimeout` / `timeout` / `writetimeout` | `SMTP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS` / `SMTP_READ_TIMEOUT_MS` / `SMTP_WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS` | `15000` / `60000` / `60000` | milliseconds; the workflow Output node's email relay (`workflow` profile). JavaMail's own default is no timeout |
| `app.tools.search.enabled` / `threshold` | `TOOL_SEARCH_ENABLED` / `TOOL_SEARCH_THRESHOLD` | `false` / `25` | on-demand tool loading ([flag](#flags-added-in-the-october-2026-sync)); deferral applies only to an agent with more tools than the threshold |
| `app.tools.search.always-on` | `TOOL_SEARCH_ALWAYS_ON` | memory, knowledge, skills, delegation and `restore_tool_result` tools | comma-separated exact tool names or `prefix*` patterns that always stay in the tool list |
| `app.tools.search.max-carry-over` | `TOOL_SEARCH_MAX_CARRY_OVER` | `6` | already-searched tools still offered directly on later turns of the same session; `0` = none |
| `app.tools.run-subtasks.enabled` | `TOOL_RUN_SUBTASKS_ENABLED` | `true` | offers `run_subtasks` to every agent that may delegate: up to 8 independent briefs run in parallel as throwaway copies of the agent (no delegation, scheduling, human-request or terminal-write tools inside a branch). `false` removes the tool |
| `app.tools.create-workflow.enabled` | `TOOL_CREATE_WORKFLOW_ENABLED` | `true` | offers `create_workflow` to an agent that lists it in its tool allow-list (the Assistant does): drafts a workflow graph from a plain-language instruction and saves it as a DRAFT. It is never activated or run. `false` removes the tool |

The edge relay ([Edge relay](edge-relay.md)) has its own table of settings (`EDGE_RELAY_*`, on by default).

### Settings that do not exist

Some recent features are controlled per tenant through the API, not by a deployment setting. There is
no property or environment variable for: the WhatsApp policy guard (declared task, opt-in records,
recipient back-off; see [WhatsApp policy](channels/whatsapp-policy.md)), or the DPDP-oriented consent
gate, purpose tags and per-contact export and erasure (`/api/tenant/privacy/**`, see
[DPDP obligations](compliance/dpdp-obligations.md)). The only deployment switch for the privacy work is
the nightly retention job (`PRIVACY_RETENTION_ENABLED`, `PRIVACY_RETENTION_CRON`, in
[Scale and reliability limits](#scale-and-reliability-limits)). The WhatsApp template send path has one
switch, `WHATSAPP_TEMPLATES_ENABLED`.

## Workflows

| Property | Env | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `app.workflow.execution-engine` | `WORKFLOW_EXECUTION_ENGINE` | `legacy` (in-process); or `temporal` |
| `app.workflow.temporal.target` | `TEMPORAL_TARGET` | `127.0.0.1:7233` |
| `app.workflow.temporal.namespace` | `TEMPORAL_NAMESPACE` | `default` |
| `app.workflow.temporal.run-timeout-seconds` | `TEMPORAL_RUN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `600` |
| `spring.kafka.bootstrap-servers` | `WORKFLOW_KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS` | empty (no Kafka client starts) |
| `app.workflow.schedule.min-interval-seconds` | `APP_WORKFLOW_SCHEDULE_MIN_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | `60`; the smallest gap between two fires of a workflow schedule. `PUT /api/workflows/{id}/schedule` with a tighter cron is a 400; `0` turns the check off |
| `app.workflow.poll.enabled` | `APP_WORKFLOW_POLL_ENABLED` | `true` |
| `app.workflow.poll.tick` | `APP_WORKFLOW_POLL_TICK` | `60s` |
| `app.workflow.poll.min-interval-seconds` | `APP_WORKFLOW_POLL_MIN_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | `60` |
| `app.workflow.poll.max-interval-seconds` | `APP_WORKFLOW_POLL_MAX_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | `604800` |
| `app.workflow.poll.max-items-per-poll` | `APP_WORKFLOW_POLL_MAX_ITEMS_PER_POLL` | `25` |
| `app.workflow.poll.max-response-bytes` | `APP_WORKFLOW_POLL_MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` | `1048576` |
| `app.workflow.poll.seen-ids-cap` | `APP_WORKFLOW_POLL_SEEN_IDS_CAP` | `1000` |
| `app.workflow.poll.failure-backoff-cap` | `APP_WORKFLOW_POLL_FAILURE_BACKOFF_CAP` | `8` |
| `app.workflow.poll.connect-timeout` | `APP_WORKFLOW_POLL_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` | `5s` |
| `app.workflow.poll.response-timeout` | `APP_WORKFLOW_POLL_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT` | `15s` |

`temporal` also needs the `temporal` Spring profile; without it core falls back
to the in-process engine. Workers run in the `core-worker` shape.

The `app.workflow.poll.*` settings bound the HTTP polling trigger (a Trigger
node's "new items at this endpoint start the workflow" binding). Polled URLs go
through the SSRF guard (`app.security.outbound.allowed-private-hosts` is the
operator allow-list for legitimate internal targets), redirects are never
followed so configured headers cannot leak cross-origin, and a failed poll
backs off to `interval * min(failures + 1, failure-backoff-cap)`.

## Cost and ROI

The per-agent cost and ROI view ([Cost and ROI](cost-and-roi.md)) has one setting.

| Property | Env var | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `roi.budget-alerts.enabled` | `ROI_BUDGET_ALERTS_ENABLED` | `true` |

It turns the ten-minute sweep that raises the 80% and 100% monthly-budget alerts on or off. The
budget itself is the existing `app.llm.gateway.budget.*` cap; this adds no new limit.
The property is read as `@Value("${roi.budget-alerts.enabled:true}")` in `RoiBudgetAlertSweep`; it is not
in `application.yml`, so the default lives in code. Compose does not pass it through (set it in `core.env`).
The `/api/tenant/roi/**` routes themselves have no switch.

## Billing gateways

Subscriptions and upgrades go through `PaymentGatewayClient`; three gateways
ship in `core/billing` — `razorpay` (always on), `stripe`, and `phonepe`
(profile `security`). Tenants are billed through whichever gateway
`app.billing.default-gateway` names; a gateway whose credentials are empty
reports itself unconfigured and a checkout attempt fails with an actionable
error instead of an opaque provider 401.

| Property | Env | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `app.billing.default-gateway` | `DEFAULT_PAYMENT_GATEWAY` | `razorpay` |
| `app.billing.stripe.secret-key` | `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` | empty (gateway off) |
| `app.billing.stripe.publishable-key` | `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` | empty |
| `app.billing.stripe.webhook-secret` | `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | empty — webhook verification fails closed without it |
| `app.billing.stripe.price-id-premium` / `-unlimited` | `STRIPE_PRICE_ID_PREMIUM` / `STRIPE_PRICE_ID_UNLIMITED` | empty (plan name fails loudly at checkout) |
| `app.billing.stripe.api-base-url` | `STRIPE_API_BASE_URL` | `https://api.stripe.com` |
| `app.billing.razorpay.key-id` / `key-secret` / `webhook-secret` | `RAZORPAY_KEY_ID` / `RAZORPAY_KEY_SECRET` / `RAZORPAY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | empty |
| `app.billing.phonepe.client-id` / `client-secret` / `merchant-id` | `PHONEPE_CLIENT_ID` / `PHONEPE_CLIENT_SECRET` / `PHONEPE_MERCHANT_ID` | empty (needs profile `security`) |

The Stripe client talks to the REST API directly (no `stripe-java`
dependency): subscription checkout uses the invoice's hosted page, and
webhooks map `invoice.paid`/`payment_failed`/`customer.subscription.*` onto
the platform's billing-event vocabulary. These are core env vars — in Compose
put them in `deploy/compose/core.env` (see `core.env.example`), not `.env`.
Billing UI also needs `WEB_BILLING_ENABLED=1`.

## Observability

| Property | Env | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `management.tracing.sampling.probability` | `TRACING_SAMPLING_PROBABILITY` | `0.1` (Lite: tracing off) |
| `management.otlp.tracing.export.enabled` | `OTLP_TRACING_EXPORT_ENABLED` | `true` |
| `management.otlp.tracing.endpoint` | `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_TRACES_ENDPOINT` | Langfuse's OTLP endpoint in the `tracing` profile |
| OTLP auth header | `LANGFUSE_OTEL_AUTH_HEADER` | empty |
| `logging.structured.format.console` | `LOG_FORMAT` | empty = plain-text pattern; `ecs`, `logstash` or `gelf` = one JSON object per line. The shipped compose (`CORE_LOG_FORMAT`, default `ecs`) and Helm charts set `LOGGING_STRUCTURED_FORMAT_CONSOLE=ecs`, which takes precedence |
| `server.shutdown` | `APP_SHUTDOWN_MODE` | `graceful`: on SIGTERM new requests are refused and in-flight ones (SSE streams, LLM calls) may finish; `immediate` cuts them |
